## Motion Authoring, Editing & Retargeting

- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-10-28, **Decompose, Don't Expand: Capability-Independent AI Systems by Construction**, Tejaswi Gowda et.al., Paper: [@2610.38144](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38144)
> **Abstract:** A recurring design choice in building AI systems is whether, when a task is too hard for a model, to expand the model (make it larger, better-prompted, fine-tuned), or to decompose the task (extract the parts that admit a deterministic solution to a host, leaving the model only the irreducibly fuzzy residue). We make three claims. First, that many tasks commonly delegated whole to a model are in fact deterministic tasks with a small generative core. Second, and our central empirical result, that decomposing such a task yields a measurable and desirable property we call parity by construction: on the extracted sub-tasks the model-size performance curve becomes flat, because no model performs them at all. We measure this across nine models spanning a 494M-parameter local checkpoint to a frontier Opus-class model, a thirty-fold parameter range on the local family alone: once the host resolves the context, operation selection scores identically for all nine models and selector resolution for eight of nine, while the one task that does not decompose, labelling, remains sloped across the same range. Because the task leaves the model, a stock checkpoint suffices: no task-specific training and no fine-tuning. Third, that this is a general design discipline, not a domain trick: we show the same move recurring across physics setup, motion retargeting, and object-model editing, and in a Web-CLI tool family (same author, so not an independent replication). We give a method for locating the cut, the construction that makes a cut exist (a deterministic surface and a closed grammar over it), and a way to make the residue safe (resolve the deterministic context; verify the generative output), position the work against neuro-symbolic AI, tool-augmented language models, and generate-and-verify approaches, and are explicit about what is measured (one domain) versus illustrative (the rest).

## Character Motion Synthesis & Animation

- 2026-11-05, **EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling**, Yanlin Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.08477](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08477)
> **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned egocentric video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning five action-video tasks. Despite adaptation only on controlled laboratory recordings, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.
- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-11-05, **IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing**, Zhoutong Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.07437](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07437)
> **Abstract:** We present IMUDINO, the first foundation model (FM) for inertial sensing to achieve competitive performance across diverse sensing tasks including human activity recognition (HAR), health, motion capture, and odometry. Prior inertial FMs often focus on one subfield of inertial sensing and sometimes underperform state-of-the-art supervised methods (e.g., in HAR), making their scope considerably narrower than FMs for vision and natural language. Moreover, they often require fixed input length and sampling rate, and generalize poorly to unseen sensor locations. To build a general-purpose foundation model for inertial sensing, we adapt the DINO self-supervised learning (SSL) paradigm to inertial data through a set of semantics-preserving motion transformations that simulate variations in motion pattern and sensor setup. For model architecture, we use a plain transformer encoder with frequency-aware positional encoding that natively supports varying input length and sampling rate. This simple setup proved remarkably effective for learning global and dense features generalizable across users, sensor setups, tasks, and, in the case of robot odometry, embodiments. We hope IMUDINO can serve as a backbone for inertial sensing and kickstart the development of general-purpose inertial FMs.
- 2026-11-04, **Semantic Residual Vector Quantization for Sign Language Generation**, Chang Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.06781](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06781)
> **Abstract:** Sign language generation (SLG) maps written sentences into signing motion while preserving both lexical content and fine-grained articulation. This requires a model to decide what signs should be expressed and to realize them through precise handshape, trajectory, facial expression and body coordination. Existing pose-based systems and reconstruction-only motion tokenizers often entangle these two decisions, leaving the generator without an explicit text-aligned intermediate target for what to sign before refining how to sign it. To address this issue, we introduce Semantic Residual Vector Quantization (SRVQ), a framework for SLG that frames generation as a two-level process moving from semantics to kinematics. The newly introduced SRV Quantizer learns a hierarchy in which the first residual level encodes sign content and subsequent levels refine pose, handshape, trajectory, and body coordination, all within a single quantizer and without separate semantic representations or external planning stages. Through word-aware conditioning for hierarchical code generation, a masked semantic generator predicts semantic codes and a kinematic generator completes the residual codes for full SMPL-X motion synthesis. We evaluate using retrieval and FID in a shared text-motion embedding space, alongside standard geometric metrics. On CSL-Daily and Phoenix-2014T, SRVQ achieves R@1 of 82.3 and 70.3, outperforming the previous SOTA by 30 and 46 points, respectively.
- 2026-11-04, **Human Motion Prediction via Continual Prior Compensation**, Jianwei Tang et.al., Paper: [@2611.06637](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06637)
> **Abstract:** Human Motion Prediction (HMP) aims to predict future human poses at different moments according to observed past motion sequences. Previous approaches mainly treated the prediction of different temporal moments as a single prediction task and learned the predictions of varied moments simultaneously, which would encounter a main limitation: the learning of short-term predictions (referring to ``near-future'' prediction) could be hindered by the predictions of long-term (referring to ``far-future'' prediction) motions. In this paper, we develop a novel temporal continual learning framework called Continual Prior Compensation (CPC) to progressively train HMP models, in which we divide the prediction task of motions corresponding to varied temporal moments into several subtasks and train the model in a multi-stage manner. To mitigate the prior information forgetting in the progressive training, we further introduce a learnable random variable Prior Compensation Factor (PCF) to explicitly measure the prior knowledge loss. We theoretically show that the PCF can be efficiently learned together with the model parameters by minimizing a reasonable upper bound of the objective function. The proposed CPC is further enhanced to estimate the prior information loss for each subtask and a new framework called Continual Prior Compensation++ (CPC++) with Fine-Grained Prior Compensation Factor (FGPCF) is finally developed. Our CPC and CPC++ frameworks are quite flexible and can be easily integrated with different HMP backbone models and adapted to various datasets and applications. Extensive experiments on three HMP benchmark datasets using multiple SOTA HMP backbones (PGBIG, siMLPe, MotionMixer, and LTD) demonstrate the effectiveness and flexibility of our frameworks.
- 2026-11-03, **Möbius: A Unified Motion-Language Model Bridging Understanding and Generation**, Jiazhi Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.05279](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05279)
> **Abstract:** We aim to build a truly unified motion model that can understand, generate, and edit human motion within a single framework. Existing motion UMMs remain only partially unified: motion understanding is handled by a language model, while generation is often delegated to a separate diffusion module operating over learned motion latents. We introduce Möbius, which brings the entire diffusion process into the same multimodal representation space used for motion understanding. Clean motion, noisy motion, and text jointly participate in shared attention, while role-specific parameters process understanding and generation streams. Importantly, Möbius operates directly on continuous frame-level raw motion, requiring neither discrete tokenization nor a pretrained motion VAE. This simple formulation naturally supports motion captioning, text-to-motion generation, temporal completion, and instruction-guided editing within a single model. On HumanML3D, Möbius achieves state-of-the-art performance among unified motion models. Scaling to 1.7B parameters and 1,700 hours of motion data further demonstrates that our framework is both effective and scalable.
- 2026-11-01, **MoPort: A View-Aware 2D Interface for Motion Language Models**, Lixing Tan et.al., Paper: [@2611.01541](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01541)
> **Abstract:** Motion language models (MoLMs) encode 3D human motion as discrete tokens for language-based understanding. Deploying them on monocular video raises two problems. Pose detection yields 2D joint sequences, leaving a dimensional gap from the 3D motion used in training. Moreover, the 2D observation changes with the camera viewpoint, while the depth lost in projection makes the 2D pose ambiguous across viewpoints. We propose MoPort, a view-aware 2D interface that enables MoLMs to take 2D observations as input. We introduce Paired View Alignment (PVA), which gives two views of the same motion a shared 3D teacher target and directly constrains the consistency of their latents, so that 2D observations from different viewpoints are mapped to a consistent semantic representation. Camera-Modulated Encoding (CME) estimates the camera extrinsics from the 2D sequence and uses them to modulate encoding, providing the 2D latents with geometric information about the viewpoint. MoPort achieves R@1 of 0.5674 on HumanML3D and 0.3286 on KIT-ML, outperforming all evaluated baselines. On HumanML3D, its R@1 decreases only from 0.5763 on in-distribution viewpoints to 0.5585 on out-of-distribution viewpoints. We validate portability on three downstream MoLMs. Furthermore, on the real-world RGB video dataset Fit3D, MoPort achieves 80.36\% accuracy. Code will be released upon acceptance.
- 2026-10-30, **Movens-Zero: Scaling Humanoid Motion Foundation Models with Large-Scale Human Videos**, Wendong Bu et.al., Paper: [@2610.41877](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41877)
> **Abstract:** Scaling humanoid motion foundation models is constrained by the scarcity of diverse, robot-executable data and by the difficulty of jointly learning from heterogeneous multimodal inputs while supporting real-time closed-loop control. We present Movens-5M, constructed from raw Internet videos through clip generation, quality filtering, semantic annotation, motion recovery, and physical refinement with humanoid retargeting and execution verification. It contains 15 million human-motion sequences and 5 million physically executable humanoid-motion sequences spanning over 10,000 hours, aligned with text, RGB video, speech, and music. Building on this data, we introduce Movens-Zero, a scalable understanding-generation-execution architecture that supports joint learning across modalities and real-time closed-loop execution. Task-mixed flow-matching training jointly optimizes a single action expert across seven generation tasks, enabling direct motion generation from text, speech, music, and video. Multimodal Real-Time Chunking connects overlapping chunks under continuously updated instructions and robot states for continuous closed-loop execution. We further introduce Movens-Intent, a four-track benchmark with 8,192 expert-verified condition-motion pairs across text, speech, music, and video. Experiments show consistent gains across all four modalities when scaling execution-verified data from 1% to 100% and action-expert capacity from 64M to 911M parameters, while demonstrating that data quality and cross-modal supervision improve generation and execution.
- 2026-10-29, **TalkFlow: Structured Latent Flow Matching for Speech Driven Holistic Motion Generation**, Xinmu Wang et.al., Paper: [@2610.40972](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40972)
> **Abstract:** Holistic co-speech motion generation aims to synthesize coordinated facial expressions, hand gestures, and body movements from speech, which is challenging due to heterogeneous part-specific dynamics, long-range temporal coherence, and cross-part coordination. Directly modeling high-dimensional motion can be unstable, while overly compact or unstructured latent spaces may lose fine-grained motion details. We present \textbf{TalkFlow}, a structured latent flow framework for speech-driven holistic motion generation. TalkFlow learns a continuous three-stream latent representation for upper-body motion, lower-body motion, and facial expressions, preserving part-specific dynamics without forcing heterogeneous motion components into a single latent space. To maintain holistic coordination, lightweight cross-stream interactions are incorporated in both latent representation learning and flow-based generation. Conditioned on multi-level speech features, TalkFlow performs speech-conditioned latent flow matching, learning a continuous transport process from source latents to realistic motion latents. Experiments on the BEAT2 dataset demonstrate strong performance across facial expression accuracy, body gesture quality, and speech-motion synchronization. Qualitative comparisons and user studies further show that TalkFlow generates more natural, expressive, and speech-aligned holistic motion than state-of-the-art methods. The project page is available at https://xinmuwang.github.io/TalkFlow/.
- 2026-10-28, **ETA-Motion: Efficient Topology-Agnostic Motion Diffusion via Sparse Joint Learning**, Seoyoung Hwang et.al., Paper: [@2610.38259](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38259)
> **Abstract:** Topology-agnostic motion generation aims to synthesize motions for diverse characters with arbitrary skeletons. Unlike conventional motion generation, which assumes a fixed skeletal template, this setting must handle diverse joint configurations and inconsistent joint semantics across characters. Existing methods address this challenge through dense full-joint modeling, which incurs substantial computational cost and hinders learning transferable motion patterns across diverse skeletal topologies. We present ETA-Motion, a topology-agnostic motion generation framework based on sparse joint learning. Instead of learning motion from all joints, our method uses a compact subset of joints to reduce redundancy and trains the motion generator on the resulting sparse skeleton. To facilitate correspondence across diverse topologies, we introduce semantic joint embeddings that combine textual and visual cues, together with topology-aware training objectives for preserving skeletal structure. Although ETA-Motion is trained only on sparse skeletal observations, it supports full-skeleton motion generation during inference. Experimental results demonstrate that ETA-Motion outperforms existing topology-agnostic baselines while significantly improving efficiency and generalization to unseen motions and unseen topologies.
- 2026-10-28, **StepControl: Long-Horizon Human Motion Generation from Foot-Contact Schedules**, Hojun Jang et.al., Paper: [@2610.38153](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38153)
> **Abstract:** Controllable human motion generation has largely focused on specifying what action should occur or where the body should move. We study a complementary question: can full-body motion be controlled by specifying only when each foot contacts the ground? We introduce StepControl, an autoregressive diffusion model conditioned on a future binary left/right foot-contact schedule, without requiring target root and body poses, or semantic prompts. StepControl represents contact as explicit temporal events and injects them through dedicated cross-attention with classifier-free guidance. We further introduce a stochastic mixture noise schedule and warmup strategy for stable long-horizon autoregressive generation. Experiments show that StepControl follows prescribed contact timing more accurately than adapting existing spatially guided motion generators to contact constraints. It maintains effective contact control over long continuous rollouts and responds to edited schedules such as cadence changes, stop-and-go pauses, limping gaits, and bilateral jumping. Our results show that minimal foot-contact schedules provide an effective and intuitive temporal interface for controllable motion generation.
- 2026-10-23, **ViT-H: Generative Video-To-Humanoid Control with Motion Tracking Feedback**, Zimo He et.al., Paper: [@2610.32966](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32966)
> **Abstract:** Learning humanoid control from monocular human videos offers a scalable way to acquire diverse whole-body behaviors. Yet, existing reconstruction-retargeting-tracking pipelines are limited in system-level scalability by separately optimized intermediate stages and cannot directly optimize reference motion executability using downstream tracking feedback. We present ViT-H, a video-to-humanoid control framework following a generation-and-tracking paradigm. ViT-H directly generates humanoid reference motions from videos with a diffusion model, bypassing human motion reconstruction and retargeting, and uses motion-tracking outcomes to post-train the generator with direct preference optimization. We further introduce an autoregressive variant that enables real-time humanoid video teleoperation with sub-400 ms latency. Extensive experiments on the Unitree G1 demonstrate that ViT-H outperforms state-of-the-art reconstruction-retargeting-tracking pipelines in both motion quality and executability. We further show that ViT-H scales favorably with model size and with both pre-training and post-training data scale, while motion-tracking feedback consistently improves executability across different motion trackers.
- 2026-10-23, **Past as State, Present as Attention: Persistent-State Blockwise Flow Matching for Long-Horizon Co-Speech Motion Generation**, Yi Liu et.al., Paper: [@2610.32373](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32373)
> **Abstract:** Co-speech motion generation remains challenging over long horizons due to the need for temporal coherence and consistent style. Existing methods typically generate long motions segment by segment, either conditioning on a short seed of recent frames, which gradually drifts, or retaining ever-longer explicit histories, which are costly to maintain and hard to learn from limited data. We trace these limitations to a key asymmetry between past and present: past motion carries slowly varying style that should be compactly summarized, whereas the current segment exhibits fast-changing local dynamics that require fine-grained modeling. This asymmetry motivates a simple Past-As-State, Present-as-Attention principle for long-horizon co-speech motion generation. Following this principle, we propose PASPA, which summarizes past history with an inter-block persistent state and models the current segment with intra-block bidirectional self-attention. We instantiate PASPA within a blockwise flow matching framework, with multi-block supervision for effective training over variable histories, prefill-decode state caching for efficient rollout, and hybrid classifier-free guidance for enhanced history and speech conditioning. Extensive public-benchmark experiments show that PASPA achieves state-of-the-art FGD performance and preserves motion style across long-sequence generation.

## Vision-Language & Action Models (VLA/VLM)

- 2026-11-05, **OopsieRecover: Learning Transferable VLA Corrections from Semantic Interventions**, Arpit Bahety et.al., Paper: [@2611.08462](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08462)
> **Abstract:** Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models can perform a wide range of robotic manipulation tasks, but often fail during deployment. Teaching them to recover from these failures requires substantial corrective data: existing approaches learn to recover from failures by directly associating corrective actions with the observations in which they were demonstrated. For instance, DAgger-like fine-tuning can require tens to hundreds of interactions, yet the resulting policy may still fail to generalize the learned corrections beyond the demonstrated scenarios. We hypothesize that semantics can capture the underlying reasons for failures and their corrections, enabling policies to learn from only a handful of corrective demonstrations and generalize those corrections to novel objects, and scene variations. We propose OopsieRecover, a framework that uses abduction to infer the intent behind a small set of corrective demonstrations and encodes the inferred knowledge as dense spatial representations and conditional costs. These costs capture when a behavior is undesirable and steer the policy toward more desirable action regions. We experiment across 6 tasks and variations in object poses, scenes, and related tasks, and find that only a handful of corrective demonstrations can enable a VLA to overcome failures across a broad range of novel situations, without requiring new corrective data for each variation. Please refer to https://robin-lab.cs.utexas.edu/oopsierecover for videos.
- 2026-11-05, **VFlash: Fast, Accurate Visual Planning via Flash Primitive Decoding**, Yu-Cheng Chou et.al., Paper: [@2611.08449](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08449)
> **Abstract:** Models for physical AI tasks, such as robotics and autonomous driving, need not reason solely in language. They can use visual plans--points, bounding boxes, and trajectories--to represent spatial goals and anticipate motion. However, autoregressive vision-language models generate these plans one coordinate token at a time, making richer predictions increasingly costly. We introduce VFlash, a method for fast, accurate visual planning through Flash Primitive Decoding. VFlash exploits the known structure of visual primitives to predict their coordinates jointly, using a lightweight drafter that generates an entire primitive in one forward pass. The layout adapts to the primitive's type, dimensionality, and length, supporting compact boxes and long, variable-length trajectories. Crucially, spatial accuracy need not require reproducing an autoregressive model's exact token sequence. VFlash therefore trains on annotated primitives and directly accepts structurally valid predictions without target-token agreement. Across the evaluated 2D and 3D robot trajectory tasks, VFlash consistently reduces prediction errors relative to autoregressive decoding. Preliminary single-rollout measurements indicate approximately 25x faster generation for 3D point motion forecasting. A world action model conditioned on VFlash plans achieves 70.2% average success across four MolmoSpaces task suites, compared with 62.6% for the baseline.
- 2026-11-05, **Edge-Assisted Crowdsensing for Explainable Cyclist Safety Perception**, Hongsen Cao et.al., Paper: [@2611.08441](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08441)
> **Abstract:** Cyclist fatality rates remain high despite policy support for active travel, and autonomous vehicles add complexity to mixed traffic. Neither crash statistics nor multi-model detection pipelines scale to continuous monitoring of perceived safety along a route. We present an edge-assisted crowdsensing system: handlebar cameras supply street-level frames, a vision-language model (VLM) turns each frame into structured traffic attributes through a single prompt, and an explainable boosting machine (EBM) combines them with OpenStreetMap road infrastructure and on-bike kinematics to predict a crowdsourced 1-9 safety score. Extraction belongs at the network edge: the fastest self-hosted model needs 2.49 s per frame and scoring takes 0.4 ms on a CPU. On 1,362 annotated frames from two London routes, the EBM regressor reaches 72.2 percent binary accuracy and a mean absolute error of 1.011 under five-fold cross-validation. It is within one accuracy point of black-box ensembles. Its per-feature shape functions give exact attribution: physical lane separation is worth +0.97 rating points, a painted lane +0.70 and a construction site -0.65. Planners can therefore price an infrastructure change in rating points, and the results enter cost-benefit analysis directly. We also report inter-annotator agreement (alpha = 0.4695), an attribute audit, and a sensitivity analysis linking attribute error to accuracy. With re-encoding, a 20 s sampling interval serves 1,000 riders at 54 Mbit/s at a cost of 1.4 accuracy points, making continuous monitoring practical.
- 2026-11-05, **Failure Prediction and Classification in Vision-Language-Action Models Via Calibrated Latent Probing**, Michal P. Podolinsky et.al., Paper: [@2611.08425](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08425)
> **Abstract:** Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) enable general-purpose embodied intelligence, but their performance is brittle under distribution shift, where unanticipated failures can damage equipment or endanger people. Deploying VLAs in real-world systems therefore requires a runtime monitor that is timely, calibrated, and informative while being efficient enough to run on the edge. Existing methods fail to fulfill these requirements. We present VLA-Tug, a lightweight probe over the latent layers of a VLA backbone that improves early failure detection and bounds the missed-detection and false-alarm rates, with minimal inference overhead. We show that supervising on the pre-failure segment of a trajectory increases early failure detection at matched false-positive rates by up to 30% over the strongest learned baseline. Under shift to unseen tasks, the bounds on missed-detection and false-alarm rates remain valid in up to 86% of runs, versus at most 21% for baselines. Unlike existing methods, VLA-Tug predicts the failure mode to enable corrective actions, raising top-3 accuracy on unseen tasks by up to 44% over a majority baseline. VLA-Tug enables real-time monitoring where the well-timed and informative probe supports lightweight steering that improves task success. We release a dataset of 3,180 annotated manipulation episodes to support future work on latent failure detection in VLAs at https://vla-tug.github.io.
- 2026-11-05, **T3R: Training-Free Two-Stage Token Refinement Towards Efficient and Robust VLA Models**, Mohammed Hassan et.al., Paper: [@2611.08413](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08413)
> **Abstract:** Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models enable end-to-end robot control, but inference is increasingly expensive. Each decision processes hundreds of visual tokens, often across multiple cameras, leading to substantial computation while allowing irrelevant background content to distract action prediction under distribution shift. We argue that these issues stem from two complementary forms of redundancy: many visual tokens are spatially irrelevant, while attention over the remaining tokens is often misallocated. Based on this observation, we propose T3R, a Training-Free Two-Stage Token Refinement framework. In the first stage, segmentation-guided token pruning removes irrelevant visual patches to reduce computation and eliminate distracting context. In the second stage, saliency-guided attention biasing reallocates attention toward action-critical visual regions and language instructions within the retained tokens. Together, pruning produces a cleaner candidate set, while attention biasing further emphasizes the most decision-relevant evidence, yielding a better accuracy-efficiency frontier than either stage alone. On LIBERO-Spatial, T3R removes 85% of scene-camera visual tokens while essentially matching the unpruned success rate, and improves success by up to 7 percentage points under out-of-distribution object-placement perturbations. The timing comparison attributes 7.8 ms per inference call to pruning and attention-bias application. As input resolution increases, LLM-forward profiling shows up to a 3.3x speedup over the unpruned baseline. Evaluations across LIBERO, SIMPLER, and RoboTwin with three VLA backbones show the strongest accuracy-efficiency benefits in multi-camera settings. These results demonstrate that training-free token refinement is a practical approach to efficient and robust VLA inference.
- 2026-11-05, **SLA: Scaling Spatial-Language-Action Model via Continual Pre-training**, Fuhao Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.08373](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08373)
> **Abstract:** Reliable robotic manipulation requires both semantic understanding and precise control in three-dimensional space. However, vision-language-action (VLA) foundations largely inherit visual representations from 2D vision-language pre-training, while spatial supervision is often introduced only during task-specific adaptation. Developing spatial understanding as a transferable foundation-model capability therefore calls for learning from broader robot experience. We present SLA, a Spatial-Language-Action Model that scales implicit spatial alignment to continual pre-training and maintains it during downstream post-training. Initialized from a pretrained pi05 policy, SLA jointly optimizes action prediction and alignment between intermediate visual embeddings and spatial representations from a frozen 3D perception model. We curate and unify heterogeneous robot datasets to support spatial learning across diverse embodiments, tasks, and scenes. To accommodate this diversity, we introduce feature-dependent gates that adaptively aggregate visual embeddings from multiple VLA layers for spatial supervision. The teacher further exploits partially available depth and camera calibration alongside RGB observations, enabling effective use of heterogeneous geometric annotations. Probabilistic state dropout encourages reliance on visual cues and mitigates overfitting to demonstration trajectories. All auxiliary components are used only during training, allowing direct reuse of pretrained VLA weights and preserving the original inference architecture.
- 2026-11-05, **RM-VLM: From Natural-Language Task Descriptions to Vision-Grounded Reward Machines**, Roko Parac et.al., Paper: [@2611.08308](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08308)
> **Abstract:** Reward machines (RMs) have been proposed as a reward formulation in reinforcement learning (RL) to capture non-Markovian tasks where credit assignment depends on history. By representing reward functions as automata, RMs expose the task's temporal structure and allow such rewards to be handled within a Markovian framework. However, they assume a labelling function, a proposition-grounding oracle for observations, which must be manually engineered for each environment, limiting their wider applicability. - 2026-11-04, **FedIA: Instance-Aware Cross-Modal Representations for Federated Prompt Learning**, Xuanjing Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.05994](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05994)
> **Abstract:** Federated Prompt Learning (FPL) enables parameter-efficient adaptation of pre-trained Vision-Language Models across decentralized clients without exchanging raw data. However, this design personalizes only at the client level: the same local prompt is applied to all samples of a client, and the global and local prompts are combined in an input-agnostic, fixed manner. Finally, an adaptive global-local fusion module calibrates the contributions of local and global text representations for each image-class pair. In this way, FedIA shifts personalization in FPL from the client level to the instance level while preserving the semantic knowledge shared across clients. > **Abstract:** Web services provide walking directions for nearly every city, but following them reliably over long routes remains challenging for autonomous robots. - 2026-11-03, **LoopQ: Fair, Closed-Loop Evaluation of Quantized Vision-Language-Action Models**, Syed Umer Bukhari et.al., Paper: [@2611.05093](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05093)
> **Abstract:** Post-training quantization (PTQ) for vision-language-action (VLA) models promises smaller and faster VLA deployments. > **Abstract:** Reinforcement learning (RL) is promising for adapting robot policies to unstructured real-world environments. - 2026-11-03, **VIGOR: Visual Gain Ordering for Hallucination Mitigation in Multimodal Discrete Diffusion Language Models**, Junzhe Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.05066](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05066)

- 2026-11-03, **Caging Vision-Language Navigation for Legged Robots via Barrier-Certified Safety Architecture**, Abhijeet M. Kulkarni et.al., Paper: [@2611.04972](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04972)
> **Abstract:** By harnessing the inherent commonsense capabilities of pretrained Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs), current Vision-Language Action(VLA) models for legged robots excel at interpreting language-based navigation commands but lack the physical grounding and explicit safety assurance required for real-world operation. - 2026-11-03, **Learning Where to Look: Observation Policy Optimization for Thinking with Images**, Junfeng Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.04943](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04943)
> **Abstract:** Complex visual question answering often requires vision-language models to think with images, i.e., actively acquire local visual evidence by zooming into informative regions of high-resolution images. - 2026-11-03, **VISTA: Vision-Language Integrated Semantic Triage Architecture for Online Face Clustering in Long Videos**, Jiashu Liao et.al., Paper: [@2611.04932](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04932)
> **Abstract:** Clustering faces by identity in long, continuous video streams is a challenging task that serves as a building block for human-centric video analysis. > **Abstract:** Large vision-language models (LVLMs) can process interleaved text and multiple images in a single context, but how they internally identify the image relevant to a question remains poorly understood. - 2026-11-03, **OCR in the Foundation Model Era: From Specialist Models to Unified Models**, Zhiheng Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.04874](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04874)
> **Abstract:** Optical Character Recognition (OCR) is among the oldest and most widely deployed applications of artificial intelligence. This survey reviews its development, focusing on the Foundation Model Era. - 2026-11-03, **Trusting Imagined Futures: Auditing Imagined Rewards for VLA Post-Training**, Zicheng Kong et.al., Paper: [@2611.04873](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04873)
> **Abstract:** Video world models enable candidate actions to be evaluated through imagined outcomes, but high predicted rewards can favor trajectories with poor execution quality. ## World Models & Generative Video

- 2026-11-05, **EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling**, Yanlin Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.08477](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08477)
> **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned egocentric video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning five action-video tasks. Despite adaptation only on controlled laboratory recordings, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.
- 2026-11-05, **ManimCode: Learning Visual Self-Correction for Educational Animation**, Zhengyang Liang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08294](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08294)
> **Abstract:** Manim supports precise, programmatic video generation, but a successfully rendered educational animation can still have visual defects, missing explanations, or incorrect content. Rendering does not directly identify the code changes needed to correct these visual failures, so feedback-driven revision is needed. Existing agentic frameworks use iterative rendering and revision but typically rely on large models, often proprietary. Existing Manim datasets primarily contain final programs and often lack quality verification and the trajectories of feedback and revisions needed to learn this refinement process. We introduce ManimData and ManimCode, a data and training framework for feedback-driven educational animation generation. We construct ManimData through a render-guided pipeline that iteratively generates, executes, reviews, and revises Manim programs with retrieved API documentation. Its 24,034 render-verified, quality-filtered records preserve intermediate programs, execution errors, visual critiques, and corrective revisions. We use role-conditioned supervised fine-tuning on these trajectories to train ManimCode, a single 4B model, to write, review, and revise animation programs. To improve correction of errors that differ from those in the collected trajectories, we then train the Reviser with reinforcement learning on self-generated states, using rendering outcomes and reference-free quality feedback. ManimCode achieves 96.6% and 97.0% render success on ManimData-test and ManimBench, compared with 52.6% and 82.0% for the untrained 4B model under the same inference setup, while also improving video quality scores from 2.48 to 2.94 and from 3.00 to 3.44. > **Abstract:** World-action models adapt pretrained video generators, and vision-language-action models build on pretrained vision-language backbones; both tie policy architecture and inference cost to large transformers designed for generation rather than control. - 2026-11-05, **Learning Robotic World Models with Semantic and Visual Feedback**, Hao Wu et.al., Paper: [@2611.07850](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07850)
> **Abstract:** Robot world models predict action-conditioned future visual states to support robotic planning and decision making. - 2026-11-05, **Seeing Beyond the Next Step: World-Model-Guided Human-Like Navigation in Multi-Agent Scenes**, Seong-Eun Hong et.al., Paper: [@2611.07730](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07730)
> **Abstract:** Selecting a next motion in a populated scene is not just a matter of avoiding what is currently in the way: an action can be locally feasible yet drift onto the road instead of the crosswalk, cut into a stream of crossing pedestrians, or bottleneck a narrow passage. These failure modes are invisible from the current state alone, and acting on them requires reasoning about what each candidate action will cause. Across five held-out evaluation scenarios, this yields navigation that is simultaneously safer, more socially and group-coherent, and more goal-directed than strong reactive and learned baselines, with naive observers rating the resulting motion as more human-like. Taken together, our findings point to a different approach for bringing world-model foresight into deployable agents: not accelerating rollouts, but amortizing them into the policy so that the cost of looking ahead is confined to training. Project page: https://seoneun.github.io/-NeurIPS-2026-Seeing-Beyond-the-Next-Step/

> **Abstract:** Capturing scenes with extreme motion and high dynamic range is fundamentally constrained by trade-offs among temporal resolution, spatial detail, signal-to-noise ratio, and dynamic range under practical bandwidth, power, and quantum-efficiency limits. > **Abstract:** Few-step autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models generate videos chunk by chunk and have made efficient video generation practical. The world model is trained on data pooled across embodiments and camera mounts; each robot's ego-dynamics is a reduced-order template with identified physical parameters. A generalist parameter-conditioned policy is learned once at this junction: each imagined step rolls out the template and then the world model, and imagination training through each robot's template makes the policy body-aware. On seven simulated robots of five locomotion types, the generalist policy outperforms imitation-learning and diffusion-policy baselines on the same world model by 3.8--10.6 percentage points in success rate and matches per-robot specialists without dedicated training, while approaching its parameter-conditioned MPPI teacher within 1.4--2.8 points without an online map. ## Computational Design, Mechanism & Fabrication

> **Abstract:** Treating morphology and control as separate design problems has constrained the efficiency and adaptability of quadrupedal robots, while current co-design approaches are often constrained by restricted design spaces. ## Sketch-based & Creative HCI Tools

> **Abstract:** AI-assisted creative tools reduce the effort of authoring content, but delegating creative decisions to AI can make it difficult for authors to understand, correct, or redirect work. ## 3D Understanding: Reconstruction & Generation

- 2026-11-05, **MOMASPLAT: Dynamic Novel-View Demonstration Augmentation for Mobile Manipulation**, Sateesh Kumar et.al., Paper: [@2611.08372](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08372)
> **Abstract:** Visuomotor policies trained through imitation learning are highly sensitive to changes in camera viewpoint. > **Abstract:** High-resolution 3D Gaussian Splatting training increases pixel and Gaussian-tile intersection counts, expanding rasterization caches that coexist with projection and gradient state at peak memory. ## Embodied AI & Robot Learning

- 2026-11-05, **Latent RL Priors for Data-Efficient Dexterous Manipulation**, Yixiao Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.08458](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08458)
> **Abstract:** Learning dexterous manipulation from limited visual demonstrations is challenging because policies must acquire both perceptual grounding and precise control, while reinforcement learning (RL) in simulation can acquire rich control behaviors from large-scale interaction with privileged information. During privileged pretraining, we distill large-scale RL rollouts into a flow-matching action decoder conditioned on proprioception and latent task context. During visual adaptation, we freeze the decoder and learn a visual interface that maps images to its latent context using action supervision alone, without privileged labels or latent targets. The same decoder can further be shared across arms by learning coordinated visual contexts for bimanual manipulation. Across three simulated tasks, our method outperforms full-parameter fine-tuning of $π_{0.5}$ and GR00T N1.7 in the low-data regime; on door lifting and bimanual assembly, it surpasses full-data baselines using $10\times$ and $5\times$ fewer demonstrations, respectively. Project website: https://yixiaowang7.github.io/latent-rl-prior-page/.

- 2026-11-05, **AffordJEV: Affordance Grounding by Confirming Candidate Regions**, Jincheng Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.07896](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07896)
> **Abstract:** Affordance grounding asks which visible part of an object performs a requested operation, a basic capability for embodied manipulation and human--robot interaction. (1) An inference framework that confirms candidate regions level by level, from object to part to sub-part, with evidence passed between the levels in both directions so that the levels correct one another. (2) A System One model trained for this framework: existing System One models are built for general-purpose decisions, judge one candidate per request and are ill-suited to the framework's region questions, so ours learns directly from the requests the framework itself produces and judges all candidates of a level at once. Trained only on InstructPart and evaluated with definition-aligned operation words, outperforms chain-of-thought methods in domain and in zero-shot transfer to UMD and IIT-AFF, is better overall than existing System One models run in the same framework, and needs fewer judge requests. > **Abstract:** Language is a natural way to command robots, but converting a single instruction into a long-horizon, contact-rich hand-object interaction remains challenging: synthesized references are noisy, human-to-robot retargeting introduces embodiment bias, and fixed-reference tracking lets small errors snowball. We further show zero-shot sim-to-real transfer on real hardware, where adding the modulator yields substantial gains over a tracker-only variant. AdaDexTrack reframes language-conditioned dexterous manipulation as modulated tracking, replacing the open-loop, fixed-reference tracking with in-loop modulation that adjusts the reference, object latent, and positional target, yielding drift-resistant execution from noisy text references.
- 2026-10-24, **RBGA: Residual-Budget Guide Arbitration for Offline-to-Online Safe Reinforcement Learning in Dexterous Manipulation**, Zongyou Yu et.al., Paper: [@2610.33494](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33494)
> **Abstract:** Dexterous manipulation requires robots to leverage existing datasets for rapid skill acquisition while maintaining safety during real-world deployment to prevent damage to the robot or its surroundings. Offline-to-online safe reinforcement learning (O2O safe RL) provides a promising solution for this problem by leveraging prior experience for online improvement under safety constraints, thereby mitigating the sample inefficiency and exploration risk of learning safe policies from scratch. However, existing O2O safe RL approaches do not explicitly account for the safety budget already consumed when making decisions within an episode. Excessive cost incurred early can therefore leave little room for subsequent exploration and policy improvement. To address this limitation, we propose \textbf{R}esidual-\textbf{B}udget \textbf{G}uide \textbf{A}rbitration (RBGA), an algorithm that explicitly tracks the remaining episode safety budget and uses it to adaptively select between offline guidance and the evolving online policy. At each step, RBGA compares the cost-to-go estimates of the guide and online actions against the remaining budget, enabling step-wise action arbitration throughout the episode. We provide theoretical analysis characterizing the safety implications of residual-budget-based selection and its effect on guide-assisted exploration. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RBGA enables effective online policy improvement while maintaining strong safety performance. Our code is available at this \href{https://github.com/sii-pilab}{link}.
- 2026-10-24, **MDPO: Multi-constraint Dexterous Policy Optimization with Reinforcement Learning**, Ao Zhou et.al., Paper: [@2610.33480](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33480)
> **Abstract:** Dexterous manipulation requires high-dimensional control policies to accomplish complex contact-rich tasks while simultaneously satisfying multiple safety requirements, such as joint limits, unsafe finger configurations, arm collisions, and unstable object interactions. Constrained reinforcement learning (RL) provides a principled framework for incorporating these requirements into dexterous manipulation policy learning. However, existing constrained RL methods for dexterous manipulation primarily focus on cumulative cost constraints and struggle to simultaneously handle multiple heterogeneous constraints during dexterous policy learning. Specifically, we employ constraint-specific policy projections to map the intermediate policy that maximizes task performance onto the feasible regions defined by the cumulative cost constraint and the state-wise cost constraint. Extensive experiments on ReDMan, including safe joint control, finger safety, and grasping tasks, demonstrate that MDPO achieves superior constraint satisfaction while maintaining competitive task performance compared with the baselines. - 2026-10-23, **ORBIT: A Modular Multi-finger Robotic Hand Platform with Rotational Finger Reconfiguration**, Benito Ribadeneira et.al., Paper: [@2610.33241](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33241)
> **Abstract:** Despite the popularity of multi-fingered robotic hands for dexterous manipulation, there is no systematic platform to compare different hand morphologies against one another. - 2026-10-23, **DexPort: A Hand-Held Skill Interface for Dexterous Hand Data Collection**, Amber Xie et.al., Paper: [@2610.32969](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32969)
> **Abstract:** Collecting high-quality demonstrations remains a central bottleneck for learning dexterous manipulation. > **Abstract:** Regularised reinforcement learning (RL) has provable robustness guarantees that should ease the transfer of policies from simulation to robots, known as sim-to-real. - 2026-10-23, **SKG in Practice: OOD Generalization Cases Across FourDomains**, Jian An et.al., Paper: [@2610.32314](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32314)
> **Abstract:** This paper presents the engineering implementation methods of the SKG inference framework and provides complete case studies across four domains. This paper not only demonstrates effective configurations of SKG across four domains but also honestly reports the variance of single-run experiments, limitations of single-seed results, negative updates caused by parameterization mismatch, and practical experience of using engineering thresholds as diagnostic signals rather than hard constraints.
- 2026-10-23, **MAST-VLA: Skill-Conditioned Adaptation with Trajectory-Affordance Priors for Multi-Skill Robot Manipulation**, Jiachi Yang et.al., Paper: [@2610.32312](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32312)
> **Abstract:** Generalist robot manipulation requires a single vision-language-action (VLA) policy to execute diverse skills across scenes and instructions. ## Neural Rendering & Physics Simulation

- 2026-11-05, **4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering**, Adnan Armouti et.al., Paper: [@2611.08342](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08342)
> **Abstract:** Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. Each task relies on a downstream model trained on real data, yet radar datasets lag orders of magnitude behind vision and language. Brute-force collection will not close that gap, and existing synthesis methods (forward-only simulators, learned generators and static-scene inverse renderers) cannot fit a real capture of a moving body, leaving a sim2real gap. We present 4D Point Splatting (4DPS), a physics-based differentiable renderer that fits an explicit moving scene to a real capture. Using the synchronized camera to initialize the room and body, we represent the scene as oriented points carrying material, normal and velocity, and splat each point into range and Doppler bins. Our output is the raw complex return a real radar records, so we apply the same signal processing on the rendered data to obtain any representation for training any SOTA downstream model. We fit 235 sequences on HuPR and 100 on RT-Pose, two datasets with different radars. Our reconstructions set the state of the art, with median correlations of 0.940 and 0.830 on range-azimuth, 0.829 and 0.695 on range-Doppler, and 0.988 and 0.966 on micro-Doppler. Our renders also set the state of the art in downstream sim2real gap. On RT-Pose, a pose model trained on them reaches 21.9 cm MPJPE on real data, against 16.7 cm for real training and 127.6 cm for the RF-Genesis baseline, where both the rendered- and real-trained models learn nearly the same features. Our custom CUDA kernels fit a sequence in 5.7 minutes on HuPR and 12.9 on RT-Pose on one H100, making dataset-scale inverse rendering of mmWave radar possible for the first time. This work can enable synthetic data generation for any human-centered downstream task and model, and scene editing for data augmentation.
- 2026-11-05, **PlayGen2: Learning Real-Time Playable Worlds with Explicit 3D State**, Jihai Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.07867](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07867)
> **Abstract:** Building a playable world requires more than generating plausible video: it demands responsive control, persistent spatial structure, and coherent interactions with dynamic entities. Using generated frames as the sole recurrent world representation ties these requirements to view-dependent appearance synthesis, making it difficult to maintain long-term spatial and temporal consistency in generation and interaction. We present PlayGen2, a real-time interactive world model that separates learned 3D state evolution from neural rendering. PlayGen2 maintains a persistent world memory comprising static geometry represented by a volumetric occupancy grid and identity-indexed 3D states for the player and other entities. Within a fixed-size local region, its world-state model jointly learns action-conditioned entity dynamics, geometry completion, and entity discovery, committing updates to a persistent world memory. A separate neural renderer synthesizes first-person observations from depth and semantic projections of the world state, without feeding generated images back into state prediction. Using Doom as a testbed, we demonstrate that the model successfully learns world dynamics including geometry-aware player motion and player--NPC interactions. The framework is computationally efficient, enabling the complete simulation and rendering pipeline to achieve approximately 160ms backend latency on a MacBook Pro with an M4 Pro chip. Beyond single-player interaction, we demonstrate zero-shot deployment on unseen Doom maps, multiplayer interaction through the composition of single-player models, and programmable gameplay through rule-based state interventions. These capabilities demonstrate the transferability and extensibility of the learned game-world model. Code and pretrained model weights are publicly available.
- 2026-11-05, **Integrating Learned Priors through Prior-Space Reparameterization for Physically Based Inverse Rendering**, Hun Hwangbo et.al., Paper: [@2611.07573](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07573)
> **Abstract:** Physically based inverse rendering decomposes appearance into materials and illumination, an ambiguous problem for which learned material predictions provide useful prior information. Most existing methods incorporate these cues through auxiliary regularization losses, which alter the optimization objective and require balancing their influence against photometric fitting. We propose a prior-guided material reparameterization that uses the predicted feature relationships to restrict the optimization space while leaving the objective unchanged. We fuse multi-view material predictions onto a mesh and construct a permutohedral lattice in the resulting prior feature space. Surface points with similar predictions share material variables, which are optimized jointly with geometry and illumination using a purely photometric objective. Experiments with two learned predictors on three synthetic benchmarks and Stanford-ORB demonstrate improved material decomposition and relighting. Our method improves relighting PSNR by 1.5-2.5 dB over the strongest evaluated mesh-based baseline on Synthetic4Relight and TensoIR-Synthetic, and outperforms a regularization loss using the same prior in controlled comparisons. We further show that our reparameterization pools noisy photometric gradients, producing updates that are more consistent across sampled views. This consistency enables a shortened joint optimization schedule that takes less than a minute while retaining relighting quality above all evaluated baselines on TensoIR-Synthetic.
- 2026-11-02, **IMUdrape: Generating Garment-Mounted Virtual IMU Data from Text through Physics-Based Cloth Simulation**, Zikang Leng et.al., Paper: [@2611.02393](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02393)
> **Abstract:** Inertial sensors are moving into clothing, from sensorized sportswear to garments instrumented with hundreds of IMUs. Sewn into clothing, a sensor no longer moves rigidly with the body: loose fabric slides, twists and swings, so a garment-mounted sensor records the motion of the fabric as well as that of the body, and this extra motion differs from one garment to the next. Models for such sensors need training data recorded with the deployed garment, yet labeled data are scarce. IMUdrape synthesizes motion from activity descriptions, reconstructs the target garment as a sewing pattern from photographs, places virtual IMUs at the deployment's real sensor positions, and simulates the garment with an intersection-free contact solver, so that every simulation yields paired garment and body signals. When the garment is simulated on recorded motion, the pose error without any real training data falls by 30% to 33% relative to generated motion, and garment data remain 12% more accurate than body data. - 2026-10-31, **3D Neural Rendering for Automated Ultrasonic Laser Scans**, Thomas Pierson et.al., Paper: [@2611.00527](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00527)
> **Abstract:** Laser doppler vibrometer (LDV) scans can be used to detect defects and cracks in manufactured parts before they are deployed. - 2026-10-30, **SketchForge: Training-Free Scene-to-Vector Sketching via Decoupled Structure and Texture Optimization**, Quang-Thinh Nguyen et.al., Paper: [@2611.00253](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00253)
> **Abstract:** Converting a raster image into a resolution-independent, stroke-editable vector sketch while faithfully preserving both structural contours and tonal texture remains challenging: raster-space rendering pipelines are difficult to edit at the stroke level, while generation-from-scratch vectorization methods struggle to remain faithful to an arbitrary input image. Project page: https://khangcntn123.github.io/SketchForge
- 2026-10-27, **ActiveAlign: Aligning Egocentric Manipulation via Active Perception**, Ri-Zhao Qiu et.al., Paper: [@2610.36859](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36859)
> **Abstract:** Real-to-Sim-to-Real (Real2Sim2Real) enables structured world representations for planning, interpretability, and safe execution. - 2026-10-26, **SceneAgent: An Agentic Pipeline from 3D and 4D Captures to Simulation Environments**, Luke Hollis et.al., Paper: [@2610.34745](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34745)
> **Abstract:** Visual captures provide detailed scene appearance, but converting them into interactive simulation environments requires object reconstruction, physical modeling, and correction of intermediate errors. We present SceneAgent, an agentic pipeline that coordinates these steps through simulator-based visual inspection and repair. - 2026-10-23, **Towards Effective and Transferable Physical Camouflage against Multi-View BEV-based 3D Perception in Autonomous Driving**, Linye Lyu et.al., Paper: [@2610.32230](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32230)
> **Abstract:** Modern autonomous driving systems widely adopt multi-view Bird's-Eye-View (BEV) based 3D perception models due to their superior performance. ## Narrative & Long-form Audio

- 2026-10-30, **MFlowAudio: Efficient Text-to-Audio Synthesis via Mamba-based Stateful Flow Matching**, Hao Dai et.al., Paper: [@2610.41852](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41852)
> **Abstract:** Recent advancements in audio generation have been largely driven by Transformer-based diffusion models. However, these models suffer from quadratic complexity of self-attention, severely bottlenecking the long-form audio synthesis. To overcome this limitation, we propose MFlowAudio, a novel latent audio generation framework that synergizes the continuous-time dynamics of Flow Matching with a custom-designed TCMamba backbone. TCMamba uses an innovative dual-scan mechanism: a TimeMamba module to capture long-range causal dependencies with linear complexity, and a ChannelMamba module to model cross-channel correlations such as harmonic structures. Exploiting this structural foundation, we formulate the Stateful Flow Matching (SFM) paradigm. This framework inherently enables chunk-wise training and streaming generation, maintaining an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ caching complexity without incurring extra computational overhead. To enable fine-grained controllable synthesis, we devise a novel guidance mechanism that neutralizes vector field collisions precipitated by on-the-fly semantic transitions of prompts. Comprehensive empirical evaluations confirm that MFlowAudio yields generative fidelity on par with state-of-the-art baselines while establishing significant computational efficiency, achieving above $14.5$% acceleration in generation and facilitating perceptually seamless streaming synthesis with a constant latency of $1.8$ seconds. Demo: https://huggingface.co/spaces/mflowaudio/MFlowAudio
- 2026-10-30, **Hierarchical Dual-Graph Retrieval for Long-form Audio Reasoning**, Eunsang Lee et.al., Paper: [@2610.41390](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41390)
> **Abstract:** Large audio language models (LALMs) struggle with long-form audio reasoning, as processing entire recordings often limits to capture query-relevant content distributed across distant segments while incurring substantial computational cost. We introduce AudioHelix, a hierarchical DualGraph framework tailored for LALMs' retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems. Unlike existing text-only or non-graph RAG approaches, AudioHelix connects related textual and acoustic evidence across temporally distant segments for multi-hop reasoning. Extensive experiments on TraceAV-Bench, MMAU-Pro, and AudioMarathon demonstrate that AudioHelix consistently outperforms full-audio processing and existing RAG methods. The project site is https://github.com/ewha-mmai/audiohelix.
- 2026-10-28, **A Tutorial on Generative Models for Music Audio**, Mateusz Modrzejewski et.al., Paper: [@2610.38252](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38252)
> **Abstract:** Music audio generation spans instrumental clips, full songs with vocals, edits of existing recordings, and systems that play alongside a musician in real time. This monograph explains the methods behind these systems for readers familiar with neural-network training who are new to music generation. It introduces neural audio codecs and autoencoders, autoregressive and masked token models, diffusion and flow matching, sampling acceleration, and preference optimization in a shared notation. Worked examples connect training objectives and sampling procedures to published systems. The application sections relate the musical requirements of instrumental generation, songs, editing, and live performance to model design. Comparison tables record representations, methods, inputs, tasks, and release information documented in papers and official sources. Symbolic composition, speech, and sound effects are included where they connect to music audio. We explain how to interpret automatic metrics and learned judges, design and report listening tests, and assess evidence about data provenance, memorization, attribution, and output detection. Selected legal decisions and disputes illustrate the implications for training and release. The reviewed systems combine established generative paradigms with new representations, planning stages, and post-training procedures. Differences in data, scale, and evaluation limit what cross-system comparisons can establish about the sources of reported gains.
- 2026-10-27, **Partially Edited Audio: Generation, Defense, and Open Problems**, Lin Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2610.37640](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37640)
> **Abstract:** Recent advances in generative audio models facilitate increasingly flexible and selective editing of linguistic content and paralinguistic, extralinguistic, and other attributes. This paradigm, named partially edited audio, differs from full audio generation as only portions of the real recording are edited. This technique can serve both beneficial and harmful applications. We therefore present a concise survey from both generation and defense perspectives, focusing on audio, as a contribution to the ICASSP 2027 special session entitled "Partially Edited Media: Perspectives from Synthesis and Defense." We first define partially edited audio and argue that what counts as ``fake'' or harmful is an application-dependent judgment, not a fixed property of the edit. We then survey audio editing by editing target, approaches, and evaluation, and then defense methods by detection, localization, diarization, and interpretability, summarizing representative benchmarks and toolkits. Finally, we discuss future directions and open problems.
- 2026-10-27, **TAC2: Timestamped Audio Captioning as composable functions**, Swapnil Bhosale et.al., Paper: [@2610.36568](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36568)
> **Abstract:** Real-world audio applications require precise, queryable representations grounded in physical time. Yet modern Large Audio-Language Models (LALMs) increasingly favor conversational generation, producing free-form descriptions that conflate acoustic perception with higher-level reasoning and lack precise temporal grounding. We introduce TAC2, a foundation model that casts timestamped audio captioning as composable functions to construct granular, speaker-attributed acoustic timelines directly from in-the-wild audio. We model the resulting multi-granularity schema of timestamped events, speaker turns, and fine-grained speech attributes using an Instruction-Input-Output (IIO) completion format, in which each task is a function of the audio, an instruction, and an optional input. A single call decodes the full description autonomously, while composing calls lets earlier outputs or external priors, such as a reference transcript, condition later ones. Paired with frozen text-only LLMs, it yields strong spoken audio understanding across diverse reasoning backends, demonstrating the utility of decoupling fine-grained acoustic perception from downstream reasoning. - 2026-10-23, **Melody Guidance for Audio Diffusion Models Without Retraining**, Cristobal Andrade et.al., Paper: [@2610.32211](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32211)
> **Abstract:** Controlling melody in audio generation normally requires training a conditional generative model on audio paired with pitch curves or MIDI notes. We instead guide an existing unconditional diffusion model, originally trained without melody inputs, to follow a target melody without retraining or modifying it. During inference, a differentiable pitch detector steers the sampled audio toward the desired pitches, while the diffusion model keeps it close to the distribution of realistic audio learned during training. Our main contribution is a sampling method that balances these two directions, providing melody control while preserving audio quality. To improve stability, we initialize sampling with a simple synthesized version of the target melody rather than pure noise. Experiments on piano, guitar, and singing voice show melody accuracy comparable to conditional models trained specifically for melody control. Our method therefore adds melody control to existing audio diffusion models without requiring paired melody data or task-specific retraining.
